## §2.2 The Inverse of a Matrix {#2-2-the-inverse-of-a-matrix}

### 2.2.1 {#2-2-1}

Find the inverse of the matrix.$\begin{bmatrix} 4 & 6 \\ 8 & 7 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The inverse matrix is$\begin{bmatrix} -\frac{7}{20} & three tenths \\ two fifths & -one fifth \end{bmatrix}$- 7 20 3 10 2 5 - 1 5. (Type an integer or simplified fraction for each matrix element.)

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.3 {#2-2-3}

Find the inverse of the matrix.$\begin{bmatrix} 7 & 7 \\ -6 & -7 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The inverse matrix is$\begin{bmatrix} 1 & 1 \\ -six sevenths & -1 \end{bmatrix}$ - 6 7 -1. (Type an integer or simplified fraction for each matrix element.)

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.4 {#2-2-4}

Find the inverse of the matrix.$\begin{bmatrix} -5 & -4 \\ 3 & 2 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The inverse matrix is$\begin{bmatrix} 1 & 2 \\ -three halves & -five halves \end{bmatrix}$ - 3 2 - 5 2. (Type an integer or simplified fraction for each matrix element.)

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.9 {#2-2-9}

Let $A = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 2 \\ 8 & 18 \end{bmatrix}$,$\mathbf{b}_{1}$=$\begin{bmatrix} 0 \\ 8 \end{bmatrix}$,$\mathbf{b}_{2}$=$\begin{bmatrix} 0 \\ -10 \end{bmatrix}$,$\mathbf{b}_{3}$=$\begin{bmatrix} 2 \\ 14 \end{bmatrix}$, and$\mathbf{b}_{4}$=$\begin{bmatrix} 4 \\ 22 \end{bmatrix}$. a. Find $A^{-1}$ and use it solve the four equations $A x = \mathbf{b}_{1}$, $A x = \mathbf{b}_{2}$, $A x = \mathbf{b}_{3}$, and $A x = \mathbf{b}_{4}$. b. The four equations in part (a) can be solved by the same set of operations, since the coefficient matrix is the same in each case. Solve the four equations in part (a) by row reducing the augmented matrix [A$\mathbf{b}_{1} \mathbf{b}_{2} \mathbf{b}_{3} \mathbf{b}_{4}$]

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The inverse matrix is $A^{-1}$ $= \begin{bmatrix} 9 & -1 \\ -4 & one half \end{bmatrix}$. (Type an integer or simplified fraction for each matrix element.)

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.11 {#2-2-11}

Determine whether the statement below is true or false. Justify the answer. In order for a matrix B to be the inverse of A, both equations AB = I and BA = I must be true

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**C.** The statement is true. The product of a matrix and its inverse is the identity matrix.

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.12 {#2-2-12}

Determine whether the statement below is true or false. Justify the answer. A product of invertible $n \times n$ matrices is invertible, and the inverse of the product is the product of their inverses in the same order

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**C.** The statement is false. If A and B are invertible matrices, then $(AB)^{-1}$ $= B^{-1}$ $A^{-1}$.

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.13 {#2-2-13}

Determine whether the statement below is true or false. Justify the answer. If A and B are $n \times n$ and invertible, then $A^{-1} B^{-1}$ is the inverse of AB

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**B.** The statement is false. The inverse of AB is $B^{-1} A^{-1}$.

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.14 {#2-2-14}

Determine whether the statement below is true or false. Justify the answer. If A is invertible, then the inverse of $A^{-1}$ is A itself

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The statement is true. Since $A^{-1}$ is the inverse of $A, A^{-1}$ A-1A =I =A $A^{-1}$. Since $A^{-1}$ A =I =A $A^{-1}$, A is the inverse of $A^{-1}$.

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.15 {#2-2-15}

Determine whether the statement below is true or false. Justify the answer. If $A = \begin{bmatrix} a & b \\ c & d \end{bmatrix}$and ab - cd not $= 0$, then A is invertible

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The statement is false. If ad - -bc not = ≠0, then A is invertible.

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.16 {#2-2-16}

Determine whether the statement below is true or false. Justify the answer. If $A = \begin{bmatrix} a & b \\ c & d \end{bmatrix}$and ad = bc, then A is not invertible

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**C.** The statement is true. If ad =bc then ad - -bc =0, and $\frac{1}{ad -$ bc}$\begin{bmatrix} d & -b \\ -c & a \end{bmatrix}$ ad-bc d -b -c a is undefined.

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.17 {#2-2-17}

Determine whether the statement below is true or false. Justify the answer. If A is an invertible $n \times n$ matrix, then the equation A$\mathbf{x}$=$\mathbf{b}$is consistent for each$\mathbf{b}$in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The statement is true. Since A is invertible, $A^{-1}$ $\mathbf{b}$ exists for all$\mathbf{b}$ in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$. Define$\mathbf{x} x = A^{-1}$ A-1$\mathbf{b}$. Then A$\mathbf{x} x = \mathbf{b}$b.

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.19 {#2-2-19}

Determine whether the statement below is true or false. Justify the answer. Each elementary matrix is invertible

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The statement is true. Since each elementary matrix corresponds to a row operation, and every row operation is reversible, every elementary matrix has an inverse matrix.

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.25 {#2-2-25}

Suppose A, B, and C are invertible $n \times n$ matrices. Show that ABC is also invertible by introducing a matrix D such that (ABC)D = I and D(ABC) = I

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**C.** $A^{-1}$, $B^{-1}$, and $C^{-1}$ exist.

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.31 {#2-2-31}

Explain why the columns of an $n \times n$ matrix A are linearly independent when A is invertible

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**B.** If A is invertible, then the equation A$\mathbf{x} x = \mathbf{0}$0 has the unique solution$\mathbf{x} x = \mathbf{0}$0. Since A$\mathbf{x} x = \mathbf{0}$0 has only the trivial solution, the columns of A must be linearly independent.

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.32 {#2-2-32}

Explain why the columns of an $n \times n$ matrix A span $\mathbb{R}^{n}$ when A is invertible

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**C.** Since A is invertible, for each$\mathbf{b}$ in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$ the equation A$\mathbf{x} x = \mathbf{b}$b has a unique solution. Since the equation A$\mathbf{x} x = \mathbf{b}$b has a solution for all$\mathbf{b}$ in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$, the columns of A span $\mathbb{R}^{n}$.

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.39 {#2-2-39}

Find the inverse of the matrix, if it exists. Use the algorithm for finding $A^{-1}$ by row reducing A I ]. $A = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 6 \\ 5 & 29 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The inverse matrix is $A^{-1}$ $= \begin{bmatrix} -29 & 6 \\ 5 & -1 \end{bmatrix}$. (Type an integer or simplified fraction for each matrix element.)

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.40 {#2-2-40}

Find the inverse of the matrix, if it exists. Use the algorithm for finding $A^{-1}$ by row reducing A I. $A = \begin{bmatrix} 4 & 3 \\ 2 & 7 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The inverse matrix is $A^{-1}$ $= \begin{bmatrix} \frac{7}{22} & -\frac{3}{22} \\ -one eleventh & two elevenths \end{bmatrix}$ - 3 22 - 1 11 2 11. (Type an integer or simplified fraction for each matrix element.)

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.41 {#2-2-41}

Find the inverse of the given matrix, if it exists. Use the algorithm for finding $A^{-1}$ by row reducing A I. $A = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 0 & -2 \\ -3 & 1 & 4 \\ 3 & 2 & 4 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The inverse matrix is $A^{-1}$ $= \begin{bmatrix} -two sevenths & -two sevenths & one seventh \\ \frac{12}{7} & five sevenths & one seventh \\ -nine fourteenths & -one seventh & one fourteenth \end{bmatrix}$- 2

</div>
</details>

### 2.2.42 {#2-2-42}

Find the inverse of the given matrix, if it exists. $A = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & -2 & 1 \\ 3 & -5 & 2 \\ -2 & 5 & -3 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**B.** The matrix A does not have an inverse.

</div>
</details>

## §2.3 Characterizations of Invertible Matrices {#2-3-characterizations-of-invertible-matrices}

### 2.3.1 {#2-3-1}

Determine if the matrix below is invertible. Use as few calculations as possible. Justify your answer.$\begin{bmatrix} 4 & 3 \\ -3 & -6 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The matrix is invertible because its determinant is not zero.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.3 {#2-3-3}

Determine if the matrix below is invertible. Use as few calculations as possible. Justify your answer.$\begin{bmatrix} 4 & 0 & 0 \\ -3 & -5 & 0 \\ 7 & 6 & -3 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**B.** The matrix is invertible. The given matrix has three pivot positions.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.4 {#2-3-4}

Determine if the matrix below is invertible. Use as few calculations as possible. Justify your answer.$\begin{bmatrix} 4 & 0 & -4 \\ 2 & 0 & 5 \\ -4 & 0 & 6 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The matrix is not invertible. If the given matrix is A, the columns of A do not form a linearly independent set.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.5 {#2-3-5}

Determine if the matrix below is invertible. Use as few calculations as possible. Justify your answer.$\begin{bmatrix} 0 & 3 & -4 \\ 2 & 0 & 2 \\ -8 & -9 & 4 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The matrix is not invertible. If the given matrix is A, A is not row equivalent to the $n \times n$ identity matrix.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.6 {#2-3-6}

Determine if the matrix below is invertible. Use as few calculations as possible. Justify your answer.$\begin{bmatrix} 1 & -4 & -6 \\ 0 & 4 & 2 \\ -4 & 13 & 0 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The matrix is invertible. The given matrix has 3 pivot positions.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.8 {#2-3-8}

Determine if the matrix below is invertible. Use as few calculations as possible. Justify your answer.$\begin{bmatrix} 3 & 5 & 7 & 5 \\ 0 & 1 & 4 & 6 \\ 0 & 0 & 2 & 8 \\ 0 & 0 & 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**C.** The matrix is invertible. The given matrix has 4 pivot positions.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.11 {#2-3-11}

For this exercise assume that the matrices are all $n \times$ n. The statement in this exercise is an implication of the form "If "statement 1", then "statement 2"." Mark an implication as True if the truth of "statement 2" always follows whenever "statement 1" happens to be true. Mark the implication as False if "statement 2" is false but "statement 1" is true. Justify your answer. If the equation $A x = 0$ has only the trivial solution, then A is row equivalent to the $n \times n$ identity matrix

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**B.** The statement is true. By the Invertible Matrix Theorem, if the equation Ax =0 has only the trivial solution, then the matrix is invertible. Thus, A must also be row equivalent to the $n \times n$ identity matrix.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.13 {#2-3-13}

For this exercise assume that the matrices are all $n \times$ n. The statement in this exercise is an implication of the form "If "statement 1", then "statement 2"." Mark an implication as True if the truth of "statement 2" always follows whenever "statement 1" happens to be true. Mark the implication as False if "statement 2" is false but "statement 1" is true. Justify your answer. If the columns of A span $\mathbb{R}^{n}$, then the columns are linearly independent

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The statement is true. The Invertible Matrix Theorem states that if the columns of A span $\mathbb{R}^{n}$, then matrix A is invertible. Therefore, the columns are linearly independent.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.14 {#2-3-14}

For this exercise assume that the matrices are all $n \times$ n. The statement in this exercise is an implication of the form "If "statement 1", then "statement 2"." Mark an implication as True if the truth of "statement 2" always follows whenever "statement 1" happens to be true. Mark the implication as False if "statement 2" is false but "statement 1" is true. Justify your answer. If the columns of A are linearly independent, then the columns of A span $\mathbb{R}^{n}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**C.** The statement is true. By the Invertible Matrix Theorem, if the columns of A are linearly independent, then the columns of A must span $\mathbb{R}^{n}$.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.15 {#2-3-15}

For this exercise assume that the matrices are all $n \times$ n. The statement in this exercise is an implication of the form "If "statement 1", then "statement 2"." Mark an implication as True if the truth of "statement 2" always follows whenever "statement 1" happens to be true. Mark the implication as False if "statement 2" is false but "statement 1" is true. Justify your answer. If A is an $n \times n$ matrix, then the equation A$\mathbf{x}$=$\mathbf{b}$has at least one solution for each$\mathbf{b}$in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The statement is false. By the Invertible Matrix Theorem, A$\mathbf{x} x = \mathbf{b}$b has at least one solution for each$\mathbf{b}$ in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$ only if a matrix is invertible.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.16 {#2-3-16}

For this exercise assume that the matrices are all $n \times$ n. The statement in this exercise is an implication of the form "If "statement 1", then "statement 2"." Mark an implication as True if the truth of "statement 2" always follows whenever "statement 1" happens to be true. Mark the implication as False if "statement 2" is false but "statement 1" is true. Justify your answer. If the equation $A x = b$ has at least one solution for each b in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$, then the solution is unique for each b

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The statement is true. By the Invertible Matrix Theorem, if Ax =b has at least one solution for each b in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$, then matrix A is invertible. If A is invertible, then according to the invertible matrix theorem the solution is unique for each b.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.17 {#2-3-17}

For this exercise assume that the matrices are all $n \times$ n. The statement in this exercise is an implication of the form "If "statement 1", then "statement 2"." Mark an implication as True if the truth of "statement 2" always follows whenever "statement 1" happens to be true. Mark the implication as False if "statement 2" is false but "statement 1" is true. Justify your answer. If the equation $A x = 0$ has a nontrivial solution, then A has fewer than n pivot positions

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The statement is true. By the Invertible Matrix Theorem, if the equation Ax =0 has a nontrivial solution, then matrix A is not invertible. Therefore, A has fewer than n pivot positions.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.20 {#2-3-20}

For this exercise assume that the matrices are all $n \times$ n. The statement in this exercise is an implication of the form "If "statement 1", then "statement 2"." Mark an implication as True if the truth of "statement 2" always follows whenever "statement 1" happens to be true. Mark the implication as False if "statement 2" is false but "statement 1" is true. Justify your answer. If there is a b in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$ such that the equation $A x = b$ is inconsistent, then the transformation$\mathbf{x}$maps to A$\mathbf{x}$is not one-to-one

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The statement is true. According to the Invertible Matrix Theorem, if there is a b in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$ such that the equation Ax =b is inconsistent, then equation Ax =b does not have at least one solution for each b in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$ and this makes A not invertible.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.21 {#2-3-21}

An $m \times n$ upper triangular matrix is one whose entries below the main diagonal are zeros, as is shown in the matrix to the right. When is a square upper triangular matrix invertible? Justify your answer.$\begin{bmatrix} 3 & 4 & 7 & 4 \\ 0 & 1 & 4 & 6 \\ 0 & 0 & 2 & 8 \\ 0 & 0 & 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** A square upper triangular matrix is invertible when all entries on its main diagonal are nonzero. If all of the entries on its main diagonal are nonzero, then the $n \times$ ×n matrix has n pivot positions.

</div>
</details>

### 2.3.30 {#2-3-30}

Suppose H is an $n \times n$ matrix. If the equation $H x = c$ is inconsistent for some c in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$, what can you say

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**C.** The statement that Hx =c is inconsistent for some c is equivalent to the statement that Hx =c has no solution for some c. From this, all of the statements in the Invertible Matrix Theorem are false, including the statement that Hx =0 has only the trivial solution. Thus, Hx =0 has a nontrivial solution.

</div>
</details>

## §3.1 Introduction to Determinants {#3-1-introduction-to-determinants}

### 3.1.1 {#3-1-1}

Compute the determinant using a cofactor expansion across the first row. Also compute the determinant by a cofactor expansion down the second column.$\begin{bmatrix} 3 & 0 & 4 \\ 3 & 3 & 3 \\ 0 & 5 & -1 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**B.** Using this expansion, the determinant is (3)(-18) -(0)(-3) +(4)(15) $= 6$.

</div>
</details>

### 3.1.3 {#3-1-3}

Compute the determinant using a cofactor expansion across the first row. Also compute the determinant by a cofactor expansion down the second column. $\left| \begin{bmatrix} 3 & -3 & 8 \\ 8 & 5 & 3 \\ 5 & 8 & -5 \end{bmatrix} \right|$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**B.** Using this expansion, the determinant is (3)(-49) -(-3)(-55) +(8)(39).

</div>
</details>

### 3.1.4 {#3-1-4}

Compute the determinant using a cofactor expansion across the first row. Also compute the determinant by a cofactor expansion down the second column. $\left| \begin{bmatrix} 2 & -2 & 4 \\ 3 & 1 & 3 \\ 1 & 5 & -1 \end{bmatrix} \right|$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**C.** Using this expansion, the determinant is (2)(-16) -(-2)(-6) +(4)(14) $= 12$.

</div>
</details>

### 3.1.5 {#3-1-5}

Compute the determinant of the following matrix using a cofactor expansion across the first row. $\left| \begin{bmatrix} 3 & 7 & -2 \\ 2 & 0 & 6 \\ 4 & 5 & 3 \end{bmatrix} \right|$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** Using this expansion, the determinant is (3)(-30) -(7)(-18) +(-2)($10 10) = 16$.

</div>
</details>

### 3.1.9 {#3-1-9}

Compute the determinant by cofactor expansion. At each step, choose a row or column that involves the least amount of computation. $\left| \begin{bmatrix} 3 & 0 & 0 & 4 \\ 2 & 7 & 2 & -2 \\ 3 & 0 & 0 & 0 \\ 1 & 2 & 1 & 7 \end{bmatrix} \right|$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">

<div class="callout-content">

$\left| \begin{bmatrix} 3 & 0 & 0 & 4 \\ 2 & 7 & 2 & -2 \\ 3 & 0 & 0 & 0 \\ 1 & 2 & 1 & 7 \end{bmatrix} \right| = 36$ (Simplify your answer.)

</details>

Compute the determinant by cofactor expansion. At each step, choose a row or column that involves the least amount of computation. $\left| \begin{bmatrix} 2 & 1 & -7 & 4 \\ 0 & -4 & 2 & -1 \\ 0 & 0 & 1 & 7 \\ 0 & 0 & 0 & 5 \end{bmatrix} \right|$

Compute the determinant by cofactor expansion. At each step, choose a row or column that involves the least amount of computation.$\begin{bmatrix} 3 & 0 & -8 & 3 & -6 \\ 0 & 0 & 4 & 0 & 0 \\ 8 & 3 & -6 & 4 & -8 \\ 5 & 0 & 4 & 4 & -4 \\ 0 & 0 & 7 & -1 & 5 \end{bmatrix}$

Use this method to compute the following determinant.$\begin{bmatrix} 1 & 2 & -5 \\ -5 & 4 & 2 \\ -2 & -4 & -3 \end{bmatrix}$- minus $- +$ plus $+ a_{11} a_{12} a_{13} a_{11} a_{12} a_{21} a_{22} a_{23} a_{21} a_{22} a_{31} a_{32} a_{33} a_{31} a_{32}$

### 3.1.21 {#3-1-21}

Explore the effects of an elementary row operation on the determinant of a matrix. State the row operation and describe how it affects the determinant.$\begin{bmatrix} 4 & 8 \\ 9 & 2 \end{bmatrix}$,$\begin{bmatrix} 4 & 8 \\ 9 + 4 k & 2 + 8 k \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">

</details>

</details>

### 3.1.29 {#3-1-29}

Compute the determinant of the following elementary matrix.$\begin{bmatrix} 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & z & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

$\begin{bmatrix} 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & z & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix} = z$ (Simplify your answer.)

</div>
</details>

### 3.1.37 {#3-1-37}

Let $A = \begin{bmatrix} 4 & 2 \\ 3 & 1 \end{bmatrix}$. Write 3 A. Is det (3 A) equal to 3 det (A) ?

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** No, det (3 A) det(3A) $\ne 3$ det (A) 3det(A). The value of det (3 A) det(3A) is -18, whereas the value of 3 det (A) 3det(A) is -6.

</div>
</details>

## §3.2 Properties of Determinants {#3-2-properties-of-determinants}

### 3.2.1 {#3-2-1}

State which property of determinants is illustrated in this equation. $\left| \begin{bmatrix} -7 & 8 & -1 \\ 21 & -3 & 6 \\ -5 & 7 & -6 \end{bmatrix} \right| =$ negative $\left| \begin{bmatrix} 21 & -3 & 6 \\ -7 & 8 & -1 \\ -5 & 7 & -6 \end{bmatrix} \right|$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** Choose the correct answer below. Your answer is correct

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.2 {#3-2-2}

The equation below illustrates a property of determinants. State the property.$\begin{bmatrix} 3 & -6 & 9 \\ 3 & 5 & -5 \\ 1 & 3 & 3 \end{bmatrix} = 3 \begin{bmatrix} 1 & -2 & 3 \\ 3 & 5 & -5 \\ 1 & 3 & 3 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**B.** Multiplying a row by 3 multiplies the determinant by 3.

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.5 {#3-2-5}

Find the determinant by row reduction to echelon form. $\left| \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 5 & -6 \\ -1 & -4 & -5 \\ 2 & 8 & 7 \end{bmatrix} \right|$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

Use row operations to reduce the matrix to echelon form.$\begin{bmatrix} 1 & 5 & -6 \\ -1 & -4 & -5 \\ 2 & 8 & 7 \end{bmatrix}$~$\begin{bmatrix} 1 & 5 & -6 \\ 0 & 1 & -11 \\ 0 & 0 & -3 \end{bmatrix}$Part 2 Find the determinant of the given matrix. $\left| \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 5 & -6 \\ -1 & -4 & -5 \\ 2 & 8 & 7 \end{bmatrix} \right| = -3$ (Simplify your answer.)

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.6 {#3-2-6}

Find the determinant by row reduction to echelon form. $\left| \begin{bmatrix} 5 & 5 & -5 \\ 6 & 7 & -7 \\ 2 & -3 & -5 \end{bmatrix} \right|$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

Use row operations to reduce the matrix to echelon form.$\begin{bmatrix} 5 & 5 & -5 \\ 6 & 7 & -7 \\ 2 & -3 & -5 \end{bmatrix}$~$\begin{bmatrix} 1 & 1 & -1 \\ 0 & 1 & -1 \\ 0 & 0 & -8 \end{bmatrix}$Part 2 Find the determinant of the given matrix. $\left| \begin{bmatrix} 5 & 5 & -5 \\ 6 & 7 & -7 \\ 2 & -3 & -5 \end{bmatrix} \right| = -40$ (Simplify your answer.)

</div>
</details>

<div class="callout-content">

The determinant is 306. (Simplify your answer.)

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.21 {#3-2-21}

Use determinants to find out if the matrix is invertible.$\begin{bmatrix} -5 & 0 & 1 \\ 1 & -3 & -2 \\ 0 & -5 & -3 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

The determinant of the matrix is 0. (Simplify your answer.) Part 2 Is the matrix invertible? Choose the correct answer below. Your answer is correct

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.22 {#3-2-22}

Use determinants to find out if the matrix is invertible.$\begin{bmatrix} -25 & -2 & 3 \\ -5 & 6 & 6 \\ 0 & -10 & -9 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The matrix is invertible because the determinant of the matrix is not zero.

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.23 {#3-2-23}

Use determinants to find out if the matrix is invertible.$\begin{bmatrix} 1 & -1 & -3 & 0 \\ 0 & 1 & 5 & 4 \\ 3 & -1 & -3 & 4 \\ -1 & 2 & 8 & 5 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**B.** The matrix is invertible.

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.29 {#3-2-29}

Let A be an $n \times n$ matrix. Determine whether the statement below is true or false. Justify the answer. If the columns of A are linearly dependent, then det $A = 0$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The statement is true. If the columns of A are linearly dependent, then A is not invertible.

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.33 {#3-2-33}

Let A and B be $n \times n$ matrices. Determine whether the statement below is true or false. Justify the answer. det(A $+ B) =$ det $A +$ det B

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**D.** The statement is false. If $A = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 0 \\ 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix}$ and $B = \begin{bmatrix} -1 & 0 \\ 0 & -1 \end{bmatrix}$ then det(A $+ +B) =$ =0 and det $A +$ +det $B =$ =2.

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.34 {#3-2-34}

Let A be an $n \times n$ matrix. Determine whether the statement below is true or false. Justify the answer. det $A^{-1} =$ (- 1)det A

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The statement is false. det $A^{-1}$ =(det $A)^{-1}$ -1

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.42 {#3-2-42}

Find a formula for det(rA) when A is an $n \times n$ matrix

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** det (rA) $= \mathbb{R}^{n} \times$ det A det (rA) $= \mathbb{R}^{n} \times$ det A det(rA) = rn•det A

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.43 {#3-2-43}

Verify that det AB = (det A)(det B), where the matrices A and B are given below. $A = \begin{bmatrix} 2 & 0 \\ 4 & 1 \end{bmatrix}$, $B = \begin{bmatrix} 5 & 0 \\ 5 & 10 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

Calculate det A and det B. det $A = 2$, det $B = 50$ (Simplify your answers.) Part 2 Now calculate the product (det A)(det B). (det A)(det $B) = 100$ (Simplify your answer.) Part 3 Calculate the product of matrices AB. AB =$\begin{bmatrix} 10 & 0 \\ 25 & 10 \end{bmatrix}$(Type an integer or decimal for each matrix element.) Part 4 Now calculate the determinant of the product of matrices A and B. det (AB) $= 100$ (Simplify your answer.)

</div>
</details>

### 3.2.45 {#3-2-45}

Let A and B be $3 \times 3$ matrices, with det $A = 2$ and det $B = -9$. Use properties of determinants to complete parts (a) through (e) below

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

a. Compute det AB. det AB $= -18$ (Type an integer or a fraction.) Part 2 b. Compute det 5A. det 5A $= 250$ (Type an integer or a fraction.) Part 3 c. Compute det $B^{Upper}$ T. det $B^{Upper} T = -9$ (Type an integer or a fraction.) Part 4 d. Compute det $A^{-1}$. det $A^{-1} =$ one half (Type an integer or a simplified fraction.) Part 5 e. Compute det A cubed. det A cubed $= 8$ (Type an integer or a fraction.)

</div>
</details>

### 3.3.12 {#3-3-12}

Compute the adjugate of the given matrix, and then use the Inverse Formula to give the inverse of the matrix. $A = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 1 & 4 \\ -3 & 3 & 2 \\ 0 & 2 & 4 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

The adjugate of the given matrix is adj $A = \begin{bmatrix} 8 & 4 & -10 \\ 12 & 4 & -14 \\ -6 & -2 & 6 \end{bmatrix}$. (Type an integer or simplified fraction for each matrix element.) Part 2 The inverse of the given matrix is $A^{-1} = \begin{bmatrix} -2 & -1 & five halves \\ -3 & -1 & seven halves \\ three halves & one half & -three halves \end{bmatrix}$. (Type an integer or simplified fraction for each matrix element.)

</div>
</details>

### 3.3.13 {#3-3-13}

Compute the adjugate of the given matrix, and then use the Inverse Formula to give the inverse of the matrix. $A = \begin{bmatrix} 4 & 6 & 3 \\ 1 & 0 & 1 \\ 2 & 1 & 1 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The inverse matrix is $A^{-1}$ $= \begin{bmatrix} -one fifth & -three fifths & six fifths \\ one fifth & -two fifths & -one fifth \\ one fifth & eight fifths & -six fifths \end{bmatrix}$- 1 5 - 3 5 6 5 1 5 - 2 5 - 1 5 1 5 8 5

</div>
</details>

### 3.3.15 {#3-3-15}

Compute the adjugate of the given matrix, and then use the Inverse Formula to give the inverse of the matrix. $A = \begin{bmatrix} 2 & 0 & 0 \\ -3 & 1 & 1 \\ -3 & 3 & 1 \end{bmatrix}$

<details class="callout" data-callout="success" data-callout-fold="-">
<summary class="callout-title"><div class="callout-icon"><svg class="lucide lucide-check" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><use href="#cicon-check"/></svg></div><div class="callout-title-inner">Answer</div><div class="callout-fold"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"/></svg></div></summary>
<div class="callout-content">

**A.** The inverse matrix is $A^{-1}$ $= \begin{bmatrix} one half & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & -one half & one half \\ three halves & three halves & -one half \end{bmatrix}$ - 1 2 1 2 3 2 3 2 - 1 2. (Type an integer or simplified fraction for each matrix ele

</div>
</details>

### 3.3.19 {#3-3-19}

Find the area of the parallelogram whose vertices are listed. (0, 0), (3, 7), (8, 4), (11, 11)

The area of the parallelogram is 44 square units

The area of the parallelogram is 55 square units
